\displaystyle=E_{vt}\left[e^{\int_{0}^{H_{0}}\zeta(B_{r})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r};H_{0}\in\left[t-K,t\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}(K,\delta)^{-1}E_{vt}\left[e^{\int_{0}^{H_{0}}\zeta(B_{r})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}p(t-s)_{|s=H_{0}};H_{0}\in\left[t-K,t\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}(K,\delta)^{-1}E_{vt}\left[e^{\int_{0}^{H_{0}}\zeta(B_{r})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}p(t-s)_{|s=H_{0}}\right]=c_{1}(K,\delta)^{-1}E_{vt}\left[e^{\int_{0}^{t}\zeta(B_{r})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r};B_{t}\in[-\delta,0]\right].

and the claim follows by choosing
\Cr{constY_{v}approx}:=c_{1}(K,\delta)\vee(1+\Cr{constY_{v}})
.
∎

Plugging the relation
\xi(x)=\zeta(x)+\texttt{es}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}
, into Lemma
3.7
immediately supplies us with the following corollary.

Corollary 3.8
.

Let
\Cr{constY_{v}approx}
be as in Lemma
3.7
. Then for all
v\in V
,
t>0
, on
\mathcal{H}_{vt}
we have

\displaystyle\Cr{constY_{v}approx}^{-1}e^{\emph{\texttt{es}}\cdot t}Y_{v}^{\approx}(vt)

\displaystyle\leq E_{vt}\left[e^{\int_{0}^{t}\xi(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};B_{t}\leq 0\right]\leq\Cr{constY_{v}approx}e^{\emph{\texttt{es}}\cdot t}Y_{v}^{\approx}(vt).

Using the Feynman-Kac formula (
1.10
) we also get the following result. Recall that
u^{u_{0}}
denoted the solution to (
PAM
) with initial condition
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
.

Corollary 3.9
.

Let
\Cr{constY_{v}approx}=\Cr{constY_{v}approx}(\delta)
be as in Lemma
3.7
. Then for all
v\in V
,
t>0
, on
\mathcal{H}_{vt}
we have

u^{\mathds{1}_{[-\delta,0]}}(t,vt)\leq u^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}(t,vt)\leq\Cr{constY_{v}approx}^{2}\cdot u^{\mathds{1}_{[-\delta,0]}}(t,vt).

The previous results are fundamental for proving perturbation statements in the next section, which themselves will allow us to analyze path probabilities of the branching process.

3.2
Proof of Theorem
1.2

Now we are ready to prove our first main result.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

We first assume
\sigma_{v}^{2}>0
and consider the case
u_{0}=\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}
to show the second part of the claim, i.e. that the sequence of processes

[0,\infty)\ni t\mapsto\frac{1}{\sqrt{nv\sigma_{v}^{2}}}\big(\ln u(nt,vnt)-nt\Lambda(v)\big),\quad n\in\mathbb{N},

(3.19)

converges in
\mathbb{P}
-distribution to standard Brownian motion. Because
[0,\infty)\ni t\mapsto\ln u(t,vt)
might be discontinuous
only
in
0
, we have to make clear what we mean by above convergence. In fact, we show the invariance principle for a sequence of auxiliary processes
(X_{n}^{v}(t))_{t\geq 0}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, where for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\geq\frac{1}{n}

X_{n}^{v}(t)
is the same as in (
3.19
), whereas for
t\in[0,1/n]
the term
\ln u(nt,vnt)
in (
3.19
) is replaced by
(1-nt)\ln u(0,0)+nt\ln(1,v)
, making
(X_{n}^{v}(t))_{t\geq 0}
continuous. Because the difference of the processes in (
3.19
) and
(X_{n}^{v}(t))_{t\geq 0}
converges uniformly to zero as
n\to\infty
, convergence of the processes in (
3.19
) to a standard Brownian motion is defined as the convergence of the processes
(X_{n}^{v}(t))_{t\geq 0}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, to a standard Brownian motion in
C([0,\infty))
with topology induced by the metric
\rho
from (
1.12
).

By Proposition
3.5
and Corollary
3.8
, on
\mathcal{H}_{nvt}
(recall the notation from (
2.23
)) we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}-\ln\Cr{constY_{v}}&\leq\ln\sigma_{nvt}^{\zeta}(v)+\ln Y_{v}^{\approx}(nvt)+nvtL^{*}(1/v)+\sqrt{nvt}W^{v}_{vnt}(1)\leq\ln\Cr{constY_{v}},\quad\text{ and}\\
-\ln\Cr{constY_{v}approx}&\leq\ln u(nt,vnt)-{\texttt{es}\cdot nt}-\ln Y_{v}^{\approx}(vnt)\leq\ln\Cr{constY_{v}approx}.\end{split}

(3.20)

Recall that a sequence of processes
t\mapsto A_{n}(t)
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, converges in
\mathbb{P}
-distribution to standard Brownian motion if and only if for all
\sigma>0
the sequence
t\mapsto\sigma^{-1}A_{n}(\sigma^{2}t)
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, converges in
\mathbb{P}
-distribution to a standard Brownian motion. Applying this to (
3.10
), the sequence of processes

[0,\infty)\ni t\mapsto\frac{1}{\sqrt{nv\sigma_{v}^{2}}}\big(\ln Y_{v}^{\approx}(vnt)+vntL^{*}(1/v)\big),\quad n\in\mathbb{N},

(3.21)

converges in
\mathbb{P}
-distribution to a standard Brownian motion. Further, by the second line in (
3.20
),

-\ln\Cr{constY_{v}approx}\leq\big(\ln u(nt,vnt)-nt(\texttt{es}-vL^{*}(1/v))\big)-\big(\ln Y_{v}^{\approx}(vnt)+vntL^{*}(1/v)\big)\leq\ln\Cr{constY_{v}approx}

holds. Consequently, if we can prove that

\Lambda(v)=\texttt{es}-vL^{*}(1/v)\quad\forall v\in V.

(3.22)

the claim follows from (
3.21
). To prove (
3.22
), we set
n=1
in (
3.20
) and note that
\frac{W_{vt}^{v}(1)}{\sqrt{t}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}_{t\rightarrow\infty}0

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
v\in V
, because
W_{n}(1)
converges in
\mathbb{P}
-distribution to a centered normally distributed random variable by Proposition
3.1
. Using (
3.24
), (
3.20
) and (
3.7
), we get (
3.22
).

It remains to show the claim for arbitrary
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
. Recall that there exist
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
and
C^{\prime}>1
, such that
\delta^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{[-\delta^{\prime},0]}(x)\leq u_{0}(x)\leq C^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Therefore, using Corollary
3.9
we have

\delta^{\prime}\Cr{constY_{v}approx}^{-2}u^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}(t,vt)\leq u^{{u_{0}}}(t,vt)\leq C^{\prime}u^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}(t,vt).

(3.23)

where we used that the solution to (
PAM
) is linear in its initial condition.
Thus, the convergence of (
3.19
) for arbitrary initial condition
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
follows from the one with initial condition
\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}
. This gives the second part of Theorem
1.2
.

It remains to show that
(nv)^{-1/2}\big(\ln u(n,vn)-n\Lambda(v)\big)
converges in
\mathbb{P}
-distribution to a Gaussian random variable. For
u_{0}=\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]},
this is a direct consequence of
(
3.20
) for
t=1
, (
3.22
) and the second part of Proposition
3.5
. For general
u_{0},
the claim follows from (
3.23
).
∎

In view of Corollary
3.9
, Proposition
A.3
and (
3.22
), the Lyapunov exponent
\Lambda
, defined in (
1.11
), determines the exponential decay (growth, resp.) for solutions to (
PAM
) for
arbitrary
initial conditions
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
(and not only for those with compact support).

Corollary 3.10
.

For all
v\geq 0
and all
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
we have that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\Lambda(v)=\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{1}{t}\ln u^{u_{0}}(t,vt).

(3.24)

Furthermore,
\Lambda
is linear on
[0,v_{c}]
and strictly concave on
(v_{c},\infty),
and the convergence in (
3.24
) holds uniformly on any compact interval
K\subset[0,\infty)
.

Proof.

Note that by Proposition
A.3
,
\delta^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{[-\delta^{\prime},0]}\leq u_{0}\leq C^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}
and Corollary
3.9
we have that (
3.24
) holds for all
v\in V
and all compact
V\subset(v_{c},\infty),
so (
3.24
) is true for all
v>v_{c}
. The strict concavity of
\Lambda
on
(v_{c},\infty)
follows from the strict convexity of
L^{*}(1/v)
, which in turn follows from the strict convexity of
L
and standard properties of the Legendre transformation. If
v_{c}=0
, the proof is complete due to
\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{1}{t}\ln u^{\delta^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{[-\delta^{\prime},0]}}=\Lambda(0)=\texttt{es}
by Proposition
A.3
and
u^{u_{0}}\leq e^{\texttt{es}t}
for all
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
. Thus, let us assume
v_{c}>0
from now on. First observe that
L^{*}(1/v)
tends to
L^{*}(1/v_{c})=-L(0)
as
v\downarrow v_{c}
. Indeed, due to Lemma
2.4
(d), for each
v>v_{c}
there exists a unique
\overline{\eta}(v)\in(-\infty,0),
characterized via
L^{\prime}\big(\overline{\eta}(v)\big)=\frac{1}{v}
, such that

L^{*}(1/v)=\frac{\overline{\eta}(v)}{v}-L(\overline{\eta}(v)).

Furthermore,
(v_{c},\infty)\ni v\mapsto\overline{\eta}(v)
is continuously differentiable and strictly decreasing, bounded from above by
0.
In addition,
(-\infty,0)\ni\eta\mapsto L^{\prime}(\eta)
is smooth and strictly monotone and tends to
L^{\prime}(0-)
as
\eta\uparrow 0.
As a consequence, we get that
\eta(v)\uparrow 0
as
v\downarrow v_{c}
and thus
L^{*}(1/v)\to L^{*}(1/v_{c})
as
v\downarrow v_{c}
. Therefore, we deduce that
\Lambda(v)=\texttt{es}-vL^{*}(1/v)
for all
v\in[v_{c},\infty)
. Furthermore, for all
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
, due to (
BDD
)

\begin{split}u^{u_{0}}(t,vt)&\leq C^{\prime}e^{\texttt{es}t}E_{vt}\big[e^{\int_{0}^{t}\zeta(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};B_{t}\leq 0\big]\leq C^{\prime}e^{\texttt{es}t}E_{vt}\big[e^{\int_{0}^{H_{0}}\zeta(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};H_{0}\leq t\big]\\
&\leq C^{\prime}\exp\big\{t\big(\texttt{es}+v\overline{L}_{vt}(0)\big)\big\}.\end{split}

(3.25)

Using Lemma
2.4
(b), taking logarithms, and dividing by
t
, we get that the (due to Proposition
A.3
) concave function
\Lambda
is bounded from above by the linear function
[0,\infty)\ni v\mapsto\texttt{es}-vL(0)=\texttt{es}+vL^{*}(1/v_{c})
and coincides with this function at
v=0
as well as
v=v_{c},
and hence on the whole interval
[0,v_{c}]
. Using (
3.25
) again, we infer (
3.24
) for all
v\geq 0
and
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
.

To show that the convergence is uniform on every compact interval
K\subset[0,\infty)
, for
\varepsilon>0
arbitrary we consider
\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}:=\{k\varepsilon:k\in\mathbb{Z}\},
and for
y\in\mathbb{R}
set
\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon}:=\sup\{x\in\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}:\,x\leq y\}
. Then the convergence is uniform on
K\cap\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}.

A fortiori, for
t
large enough, uniformly in
y\in K
,

u(t,t\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon})\geq e^{t(\Lambda(\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon})-\varepsilon)}.

Lemma
A.8
then entails that

\inf_{z\in[t\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon}-1,t\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon}+1]}u(t+1,z)\geq\frac{1}{\Cr{Harnack}}u(t,t\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon})\geq\frac{1}{\Cr{Harnack}}e^{t(\Lambda(\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon})-\varepsilon)}.

(3.26)

Furthermore, using
0\leq y-\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon}\leq\varepsilon
, we have

\begin{split}P_{yt}(B_{\varepsilon t}\in[t\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon}-1,t\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon}+1])&\geq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi\varepsilon t}}\inf_{x\in[t\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon}-1,t\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon}+1]}e^{-\frac{(x-yt)^{2}}{2\varepsilon t}}\\
&\geq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\cdot\exp\Big\{-\frac{(\varepsilon t+1)^{2}}{2\varepsilon t}-\frac{\ln(\varepsilon t)}{2}\Big\}.\end{split}

(3.27)

Using the Feynman-Kac formula in the equality, the Markov property at time
\varepsilon t,
and (
BDD
) in the first inequality, we infer that

\displaystyle\begin{split}u(t+1+\varepsilon t&,yt)=E_{yt}\big[e^{\int_{0}^{t+1+\varepsilon t}\xi(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s}u_{0}(B_{t+1+\varepsilon t})\big]\\
&\geq e^{\texttt{ei}\varepsilon t}\cdot P_{yt}(B_{\varepsilon t}\in[t\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon}-1,t\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon}+1])\cdot\inf_{z\in[t\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon}-1,t\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon}+1]}u(t+1,z)\\
&\geq c_{1}\exp\Big\{t\Big(\Lambda(\lfloor{y}\rfloor_{\varepsilon})+(\texttt{ei}-3/2)\varepsilon-\frac{1}{t}-\frac{1}{2\varepsilon t^{2}}-\frac{\ln(\varepsilon t)}{2t}\Big)\Big\},\end{split}

(3.28)

where in the last inequality we used (
3.26
) and (
3.27
).
Setting
{t}^{\prime}:=t+1+\varepsilon t
and
{y}^{\prime}:=\frac{{t}^{\prime}}{t}y
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{t}^{\prime}}\ln u({t}^{\prime},{t}^{\prime}y)-\Lambda(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{t}{{t}^{\prime}}\Big(\frac{1}{t}\ln u({t}^{\prime},t{y}^{\prime})-\Lambda(\lfloor{{y}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{\varepsilon})\Big)+\frac{t}{{t}^{\prime}}\Lambda(\lfloor{{y}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{\varepsilon})-\Lambda(y).

Since (
3.28
) holds uniformly for all
y\in K
, we infer that

\inf_{y\in K}\Big(\frac{1}{{t}^{\prime}}\ln u({t}^{\prime},{t}^{\prime}y)-\Lambda(y)\Big)\geq\frac{t}{{t}^{\prime}}\Big(\frac{\ln c_{1}}{t}+(\texttt{ei}-3/2)\varepsilon-\frac{1}{t}-\frac{1}{2\varepsilon t^{2}}-\frac{\ln(\varepsilon t)}{2t}\Big)-\sup_{y\in K}\Big|\frac{t}{{t}^{\prime}}\Lambda(\lfloor{{y}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{\varepsilon})-\Lambda(y)\Big|.

Since
\Lambda
concave and finite, it is uniformly continuous on compact intervals. As a consequence, since

\varepsilon>0
was chosen arbitrarily,
we deduce the lower bound

\liminf_{t\to\infty}\inf_{y\in K}\Big(\frac{1}{t}\ln u(t,ty)-\Lambda(y)\Big)\geq 0.

(3.29)

To derive the matching upper bound, we assume that the convergence does not hold uniformly on
K
. Then, due to (
3.29
), there exist
\alpha>0
and sequences
(t_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,\infty)
and
(y_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset K
such that
t_{n}\to\infty
and

\frac{1}{t_{n}}\ln u(t_{n},t_{n}y_{n})-\Lambda(y_{n})\geq\alpha\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}.

(3.30)

Retreating to a suitable subsequence, we can assume
y_{n}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}_{n\rightarrow\infty}y\in K.

For
n
such that
|y_{n}-y|\leq\varepsilon
, we have, similarly to (
3.27
),

\displaystyle P_{y(t_{n}+1+\varepsilon t_{n})}\big(B_{\varepsilon t_{n}}\in[t_{n}y_{n}-1,t_{n}y_{n}+1]\big)

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi\varepsilon t_{n}}}\inf_{x\in[t_{n}y_{n}-1,t_{n}y_{n}+1]}e^{-\frac{(x-y(t_{n}+1+\varepsilon t_{n}))^{2}}{2\varepsilon t_{n}}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi\varepsilon t_{n}}}\exp\Big\{-\frac{(1+y)^{2}}{2\varepsilon t_{n}}(t_{n}\varepsilon+1)^{2}\Big\}.

Therefore, taking advantage of (
3.26
) again and using an argument as in the derivation of (
3.28
), we infer that

\displaystyle u(t_{n}+1+\varepsilon t_{n},(t_{n}+1+\varepsilon t_{n})y)

\displaystyle\geq c_{1}\cdot u(t_{n},t_{n}y_{n})\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{\varepsilon t_{n}}}\exp\Big\{\varepsilon\texttt{ei}t_{n}-\frac{(1+y)^{2}}{2\varepsilon t_{n}}(t_{n}\varepsilon+1)^{2}\Big\}

(3.31)

for all
n
such that
|y_{n}-y|\leq\varepsilon
.
Now recall that
y_{n}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}_{n\rightarrow\infty}y
, that
\Lambda
is continuous, as well as
\frac{1}{t}\ln u(t,ty)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}_{t\rightarrow\infty}\Lambda(y).

Therefore, first taking logarithms, dividing by
t_{n}
, and then taking
n\to\infty,
the left-hand side in (
3.31
) converges to
(1+\varepsilon)\Lambda(y).
Contrarily, by (
3.30
), the limit of the right-hand side is bounded from below by
\Lambda(y)+\alpha+\varepsilon\texttt{ei}-\frac{(1+y)^{2}}{2}\varepsilon
.
Choosing
\varepsilon>0
small enough,
this leads to a contradiction. As a consequence, we deduce the uniform convergence on
K
.
∎

3.3
Time perturbation

In the next step we prove perturbation results, i.e., time and space perturbation Lemmas
3.11
and
3.12
. These statements will be useful when comparing the expected number of particles which are slightly slower or faster than the ones with given velocity. As usual,
u=u^{u_{0}}
denotes the solution to (
PAM
) with initial condition
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
.

Lemma 3.11
.

(a)

Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
and let
\varepsilon:\,(0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)
be a function such that
\varepsilon(t)\to 0
and
t\varepsilon(t)\to\infty
as
t\to\infty
. Then there exists
=\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}ert_{g}enau}((\varepsilon(t))_{t\geq 0},u_{0})
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for all
t
large enough,

\sup_{(v,h)\in\mathcal{E}_{t}}\Big|\ln\Big(\frac{u^{u_{0}}(t+h,vt)}{u^{u_{0}}(t,vt)}\Big)-h(\text{\emph{{es}}}-\overline{\eta}(v))\Big|\leq\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}ert_{g}enau}+\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}ert_{g}enau}|h|\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\ln t}{t}}+\frac{|h|}{t}\Big),

(3.32)

where
\mathcal{E}_{t}:=\left\{(v,h):\ v\in V,\ |h|\leq t\varepsilon(t),\frac{vt}{t+h}\in V\right\}
.

(b)

For all
\varepsilon>0
and
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
there exists a constant
>1
and a
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. finite random variable such that for all
t\geq\Cr{T_{i}ndex_{t}imepert}
, uniformly in
0\leq h\leq t^{1-\varepsilon}
,
v\in V
and
\frac{vt}{t+h}\in V,

\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}^{-1}e^{h/\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}}u^{u_{0}}(t,vt)\leq u^{u_{0}}(t+h,vt)\leq\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}e^{\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}h}u^{u_{0}}(t,vt).

(3.33)

Proof.

(a)
Note that it suffices to show the claim for
u_{0}=\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}
. Indeed,
for all
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
we have
\delta^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{[-\delta^{\prime},0]}\leq u_{0}\leq C^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}
. Using Corollary
3.9
, we infer that for all

u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
, all
v\in V,
and all
t
large enough

\delta^{\prime}\Cr{constY_{v}approx}^{-2}u^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}(t,vt)\leq u^{{u_{0}}}(t,vt)\leq C^{\prime}u^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}(t,vt).

where
u^{u_{0}}
denotes the solution to (
PAM
) with initial
condition
u_{0}
.

by the same argument as at the end of the proof of Theorem
1.2
, the solutions to (
PAM
) for different initial conditions
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
differ at most by a multiplicative constant.
Let
t
be large enough such that
\mathcal{H}_{vt}
occurs for all
v\in V
, which is possible by (
2.23
).
Letting
(v,h)\in\mathcal{E}_{t}
and writing
v^{\prime}:=\frac{vt}{t+h}\in V,

we infer that

\displaystyle\frac{u^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}(t+h,vt)}{u^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}(t,vt)}

\displaystyle=\frac{E_{vt}\left[e^{\int_{0}^{t+h}\xi(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};B_{t+h}\leq 0\right]}{E_{vt}\left[e^{\int_{0}^{t}\xi(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};B_{t}\leq 0\right]}=e^{\texttt{es}\cdot h}\frac{E_{vt}\left[e^{\int_{0}^{t+h}\zeta(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};B_{t+h}\leq 0\right]}{E_{vt}\left[e^{\int_{0}^{t}\zeta(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};B_{t}\leq 0\right]}.

(3.34)

Using Lemma
3.7
, on
\mathcal{H}_{vt},
the last fraction divided by

\frac{Y_{v^{\prime}}^{\approx}(vt)}{Y_{v}^{\approx}(vt)}
is bounded away from
0
and infinity for all
t
large enough.
As in the derivation of (
3.11
), the term
\frac{Y_{v^{\prime}}^{\approx}(vt)}{Y_{v}^{\approx}(vt)}
can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{E_{vt}^{\zeta,\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})}\Big[\exp\Big\{-\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})\sum_{i=1}^{vt}\widehat{\tau}_{i}\Big\};\sum_{i=1}^{vt}\widehat{\tau}_{i}\in\Big[-K,0\Big]\Big]}{E_{vt}^{\zeta,\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v)}\Big[\exp\Big\{-\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v)\sum_{i=1}^{vt}\widetilde{\tau}_{i}\Big\};\sum_{i=1}^{vt}\widetilde{\tau}_{i}\in\Big[-K,0\Big]\Big]}\times\frac{\exp\Big\{-vt\Big(\frac{\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})}{v^{\prime}}-\overline{L}_{vt}^{\zeta}(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime}))\Big)\Big\}}{\exp\Big\{-vt\Big(\frac{\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v)}{v}-\overline{L}_{vt}^{\zeta}(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v))\Big)\Big\}},

(3.35)

where
\widehat{\tau}_{i}=\tau_{i}-E_{vt}^{\zeta,\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})}[\tau_{i}]
and
\widetilde{\tau_{i}}:=\tau_{i}-E_{vt}^{\zeta,\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v)}[\tau_{i}]
. But now, since
v^{\prime}\in V,
as in the proof of Proposition
3.5
, the first fraction of the previous display is bounded from below and above by positive constants, for all
t
large enough.
Indeed, setting
x=vt
in (
3.12
), the denominator in (
3.35
) equals the integral in (
3.12
)
and, replacing
v
by
v^{\prime}
in (
3.12
), the numerator equals the integral in (
3.12
).
The claim then follows due to Lemma
3.6
and using (
3.7
). Therefore, taking logarithms in (
3.34
) and recalling the definition of
S_{vt}^{\zeta,v}(\eta)
in (
3.4
), according to the previous considerations it suffices to show that the logarithm of the second fraction in (
3.35
) plus
\overline{\eta}(v)\cdot h
, i.e.

\displaystyle\big(S_{vt}^{\zeta,v}(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v))-S_{vt}^{\zeta,v}(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime}))\big)+\big(S_{vt}^{\zeta,v}(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime}))-S_{vt}^{\zeta,v^{\prime}}(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime}))\big)+\overline{\eta}(v)\cdot h,

(3.36)

satisfies bound on the right-hand side of (
3.32
), uniformly in
(v,h)\in\mathcal{E}_{t}
.
Recall that
\frac{1}{v^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{v}\left(1+\frac{h}{t}\right)
, thus the second summand in (
3.36
) is
-h\cdot\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})
. The triangular inequality entails

\displaystyle|\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})-\overline{\eta}(v)|

\displaystyle\leq|\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})-\overline{\eta}(v^{\prime})|+|\overline{\eta}(v^{\prime})-\overline{\eta}(v)|,

(3.37)

and so by Lemma
2.5
, uniformly for
v^{\prime}\in V
and
t
large enough, the first term on the right-hand side of (
3.37
) can be upper bounded by
\Cr{const:concEtaEmpLT}\sqrt{\frac{\ln vt}{vt}}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. Furthermore, by Lemma
2.4
d) we know that
\overline{\eta}
is continuously differentiable and strictly decreasing, having uniform positive bounds of the derivative on every bounded subinterval of
(v_{c},\infty)
.
Hence,
\overline{\eta}(\cdot)
is Lipschitz continuous on
V
and we therefore get that the second summand in (
3.37
) can be upper bounded by
c_{1}|v-v^{\prime}|=c_{1}v\frac{|h|}{|t+h|}=c_{1}v\frac{|h|}{t}\cdot\frac{t}{|t+h|}\leq c_{2}\frac{|h|}{t}
, uniformly for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V
and all
t
large enough, where the last inequality is due to
|h|/t\leq\varepsilon(t)\to 0
. Therefore, the absolute value of the sum of the second and third summand in (
3.36
)
is upper bounded by
\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}ert_{g}enau}|h|\big(\sqrt{\frac{\ln t}{t}}+\frac{|h|}{t}\big)
with
\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}ert_{g}enau}:=c_{2}\vee\Cr{const:concEtaEmpLT}
.

It remains to show that the first summand in (
3.36
) tends to
0
as
t
tends to
\infty
.
We write

\displaystyle S_{vt}^{\zeta,v}(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=S_{vt}^{\zeta,v}(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v))+\big(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})-\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v)\big)\big(S_{vt}^{\zeta,v}\big)^{\prime}(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v))+\frac{1}{2}\big(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})-\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v)\big)^{2}\big(S_{vt}^{\zeta,v}\big)^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\eta})

for some
\widetilde{\eta}\in[\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})\wedge\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v),\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})\vee\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v)]
. Recall that
S_{vt}^{\zeta,v}(\eta)=vt\big(\frac{\eta}{v}-\overline{L}_{vt}^{\zeta}(\eta)\big)
and by definition
\big(\overline{L}_{vt}^{\zeta}\big)^{\prime}\big(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v)\big)=\frac{1}{v}
. Thus,

\big(S_{vt}^{\zeta,v}\big)^{\prime}(\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v))=0.

Furthermore,
\big(S_{vt}^{\zeta,v}\big)^{\prime\prime}(\eta)=-{vt}\big(\overline{L}_{vt}^{\zeta}\big)^{\prime\prime}(\eta)
and the function
\big(\overline{L}_{vt}^{\zeta}\big)^{\prime\prime}
is uniformly bounded away from
0
and infinity by Lemma
A.1
on
V.
Thus, by the characterizing equation (
2.15
) and the implicit function theorem, on
\mathcal{H}_{vt}
, the function
\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(\cdot)
is differentiable with uniformly bounded first derivative, i.e.

\big|\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})-\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v)\big|\leq c_{3}|v^{\prime}-v|\leq c_{3}v\frac{|h|}{t(1-\varepsilon(t))}.

(3.38)

Thus, on
\mathcal{H}_{vt}
, the first summand in (
3.36
) can be bounded by
c_{4}\cdot t\cdot\frac{h^{2}}{t^{2}}=c_{4}\cdot\frac{h^{2}}{t},

uniformly in
(v,h)\in\mathcal{E}_{t}
, for all
t
large enough. This implies
a).

(b)
The first part of the proof is similar to that of
a)
; indeed, dividing by
u^{u_{0}}(t,vt)
and taking logarithms in (
3.33
), one arrives at (
3.36
) again. The second part then consists of showing that (
3.36
) is lower and upper bounded by two strictly increasing linear functions for all
0<h\leq t^{1-\varepsilon}
. Following the same computations as in the proof of
a),
for
v\in V
and
h>0
, such that
v^{\prime}\in V
, we have up to some additive constant, which is independent of
h
, that

\displaystyle\ln\frac{u^{u_{0}}(t+h,vt)}{u^{u_{0}}(t,vt)}

\displaystyle\asymp c_{t}\frac{vt}{2}(v-v^{\prime})^{2}-\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})\cdot h+\texttt{es}\cdot h,

where
c_{t}
is a function which for
t
large enough is positive and bounded away from
0
and infinity. Because
\eta_{vt}^{\zeta}(v^{\prime})<0
, the latter expression is bounded from below by
h/\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}
and bounded from above by
\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}\cdot h
for our choice of parameters, hence we can conclude.
∎

3.4
Space perturbation

While in the previous section we have been investigating the effects of time perturbations of
u
and related quantities, here we will consider space perturbations.
As before, let
u^{u_{0}}
denotes the solution to (
PAM
) with initial condition
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
.

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
\varepsilon(t)
be a positive function such that
\varepsilon(t)\to 0
and

\frac{t\varepsilon(t)}{\ln t}\to\infty
as
t\to\infty
. Then for all
\varepsilon>0
there
exists
C(\varepsilon)>0
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for all
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
we have

(a)

\hskip-28.45274pt\limsup_{t\to\infty}\ \sup\left\{\left|\frac{1}{h}\ln\left(\frac{u(t,vt+h)}{u(t,vt)}\right)-L\big(\overline{\eta}(v)\big)\right|:(v,h)\in\mathcal{E}_{t}\right\}\leq\varepsilon,

(3.39)

where
\mathcal{E}_{t}:=\left\{(v,h):\ v,v+\frac{h}{t}\in V,\ C(\varepsilon)\ln t\leq|h|\leq t\varepsilon(t)\right\}
.

(b)

Let
\varepsilon(t)
be a positive function such that
\varepsilon(t)\to 0
. Then there exists a constant
<\infty

and a
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. finite random variable
such that for all

t\geq\Cr{T_{i}ndex_{s}pacepert}
, uniformly in
0\leq h\leq t\varepsilon(t)
,
v\in V
,
v+\frac{h}{t}\in V
and
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
we have

\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}^{-1}e^{-\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}h}\cdot u(t,vt)\leq u(t,vt+h)\leq\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}e^{-h/\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}}\cdot u(t,vt).

(3.40)

The proof of Lemma
3.12
can be found in the companion article
[
11
]
; indeed, it is central to the results of
[
11
]
and we hence prefer not to duplicate it here due to space constraints.

3.5
Approximation results

In this section we mainly show how moment generating functions can be used in order to approximate quantities related to the solution to (
PAM
) and BBMRE. As usual, for
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
let
u^{u_{0}}
be the solution to (
PAM
) with initial condition
u_{0}
.

Lemma 3.13
.

There exists a constant
>0
and a
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. finite random variable such that for all
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
and
t\geq\Cr{randomindexapproxtopamsppedv0},

\Big|\ln u^{u_{0}}(t,v_{0}t)-\sum_{i=1}^{v_{0}t}\big(L_{i}^{\zeta}(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))-L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))\big)\Big|\leq\Cr{constapproxtopamwithspeedv0}\ln t.

(3.41)

Proof.

By (
3.22
) and
\Lambda(v_{0})=0
, we have
L^{*}(1/v_{0})=\frac{\texttt{es}}{v_{0}}
. Also, by (
PAM-INI
) and monotonicity of the solution to (
PAM
) in its initial condition, we have
u^{\delta^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{[-\delta^{\prime},0]}}\leq u^{u_{0}}\leq u^{C^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}
. Thus, on the one hand, by the many-to-few lemma (Proposition
2.3
) and Lemma
3.7
, for all
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
and
t
such that
v_{0}t\geq\Cr{constHn}
, where
\Cr{constHn}
was defined in (
2.23
), we have

\left|\ln u^{u_{0}}(t,v_{0}t)-\left(\ln Y_{v_{0}}^{\approx}(v_{0}t)+v_{0}tL^{*}(1/v_{0})\right)\right|\leq\ln(\Cr{constY_{v}approx}/\delta^{\prime}).

On the other hand, due to Proposition
3.5
and Corollary
3.4
, there exists a finite random variable
\mathcal{N}
, such that for all
t\geq\mathcal{N}
we have

\Big|\ln Y_{v_{0}}^{\approx}({v_{0}t})+v_{0}tL^{*}(1/v_{0})+\ln\sigma_{v_{0}t}^{\zeta}(v_{0})-\sum_{i=1}^{v_{0}t}\big(L_{i}^{\zeta}(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))-L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))\big)\Big|\leq\ln\Cr{constY_{v}}+\Cr{const_{c}oncSn}\ln v_{0}+\Cr{const_{c}oncSn}\ln t.

Finally, by (
3.7
),
|\ln\sigma_{v_{0}t}^{\zeta}(v_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\ln t|\leq\ln\Cr{constSigman}+\frac{1}{2}|\ln v_{0}|
for all
t
such that
v_{0}t\geq\Cr{constHn}
.
Combining this with the previous two display, inequality (
3.41
) follows with
\Cr{randomindexapproxtopamsppedv0}:=(\mathcal{N}\vee\Cr{constHn})/v_{0}
and
\Cr{constapproxtopamwithspeedv0}
suitable.
∎

We introduce the so-called
breakpoint inverse

T_{x}^{u_{0},a}:=\inf\big\{t\geq 0:u^{u_{0}}(t,x)\geq a\big\},\quad x\in\mathbb{R},\ a\in[0,\infty),\ u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}},

(3.42)

and abbreviate

T_{x}^{(a)}:=T_{x}^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]},a}.

(3.43)

Next, we state an important approximation result for
T_{x}^{u_{0},a}
,
x\geq 0
, in terms of the centered logarithmic moment generating functions.

Lemma 3.14
.

There exists a constant
<\infty
and a
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. finite random variable
=\Cr{constprobapproxofTn}(a,u_{0})
,
a>0,u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
, such that for all
x\geq 1,

\Big|T_{x}^{u_{0},a}-\frac{1}{v_{0}L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))}\sum_{i=1}^{x}L_{i}^{\zeta}(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))\Big|\leq\Cr{constprobapproxofTn}+\Cr{constapproxofTn}\ln x.

(3.44)

Additionally, for each
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
and
a>0,

\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{T_{x}^{u_{0},a}}{x}=\frac{1}{v_{0}}\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s}.

(3.45)

Proof.

We set
t=x/v_{0}
and let

h_{t}:=\frac{1}{v_{0}L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))}\sum_{i=1}^{v_{0}t}\big(L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0})-L_{i}^{\zeta}(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))\big).

We first note that due to Lemma
A.2
, the family

(L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))-L_{i}^{\zeta}(\overline{\eta}(v_{0})))_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
satisfies the assumptions of Corollary
A.5
with all
m_{i}
equal to some large enough finite constant and thus
\sum_{n}\mathbb{P}(|h_{n}|\geq C\sqrt{n\ln n})<\infty
for some
C>1
large enough. The first Borel-Cantelli lemma then readily supplies us with
|h_{n}|<C\sqrt{n\ln n}

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
n
large enough. To control non-integer
t
, we recall

\displaystyle h_{t}-h_{\lfloor t\rfloor}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v_{0}L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))}\big(v_{0}(t-\lfloor t\rfloor)L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))-\ln E_{v_{0}t}\big[e^{\int_{0}^{H_{v_{0}\lfloor t\rfloor}}(\zeta(B_{s})+\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s}\big]\big)

and hence
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. that
|h_{t}-h_{\lfloor t\rfloor}|\leq 1+\frac{\sqrt{2(\texttt{es}-\texttt{ei}-\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))}}{|L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))|}
by (
BDD
) and
[
4
, (2.0.1), p. 204]
, thus giving

|h_{t}|<c_{1}\sqrt{t\ln t}\ \mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.\ for all $t$ large enough.}

(3.46)

To show the desired inequality, we note that (
3.44
) is equivalent to

\big|T_{v_{0}t}^{u_{0},a}-(t-h_{t})\big|\leq\Cr{constprobapproxofTn}+\Cr{constapproxofTn}\ln(v_{0}t).

(3.47)

For proving the latter, observe that it is sufficient to show that we can choose
\Cr{constapproxofTn}>0
as well as a
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. finite random variable
\mathcal{T},
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}u^{\delta^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{[-\delta^{\prime},0]}}(t-h_{t}+\Cr{constapproxofTn}\ln t,v_{0}t)&\geq a\quad\text{ and }\quad u^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}(t-h_{t}-\Cr{constapproxofTn}\ln t,v_{0}t)<\frac{a}{2C^{\prime}},\,\forall t\geq\mathcal{T}.\end{split}

(3.48)

with
\delta^{\prime},C^{\prime}
from (
PAM-INI
). Indeed, due to (
PAM-INI
), the first inequality in (
3.48
) implies
T_{v_{0}t}^{u_{0},a}\leq T_{v_{0}t}^{\delta^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{[-\delta^{\prime},0]},a}\leq t-h_{t}+\Cr{constapproxofTn}\ln t
for
t\geq\mathcal{T}
. To use the second inequality, first note that
T_{v_{0}t}^{u_{0},a}\geq T_{v_{0}t}^{C^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]},a}=T_{v_{0}t}^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]},a/C^{\prime}}
. Then, using
u^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}(s,x)=\texttt{E}_{x}^{\xi}\big[N^{\leq}(s,0)\big]
and Lemma
A.6
, (
3.48
) implies
T_{v_{0}t}^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]},a/C^{\prime}}\geq t-h_{t}-\Cr{constapproxofTn}\ln t
for all
t\geq\mathcal{T}
. For
t<\mathcal{T}
on the other hand, we can use that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., the family
T_{v_{0}t}^{u_{0},a},

t<\mathcal{T},
as well as the
L_{i}^{\zeta}(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))
are uniformly bounded, allowing us to upper bound the remaining cases of (
3.47
) by some finite random variable
\Cr{constprobapproxofTn}
.

Thus, in order to show (
3.48
), note that for
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and uniformly in
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
we have that

\displaystyle\ln u^{u_{0}}(t-h_{t}+\alpha\ln t,v_{0}t)

\displaystyle=\ln\Big(\frac{u^{u_{0}}(t-h_{t}+\alpha\ln t,v_{0}t)}{u^{u_{0}}(t,v_{0}t)}\Big)+\sum_{i=1}^{v_{0}t}\big(L_{i}^{\zeta}(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))-L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))\big)+a_{t}

\displaystyle=(-h_{t}+\alpha\ln t)(\texttt{es}-\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))+v_{0}L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))h_{t}+b(\alpha,t)=\alpha(\texttt{es}-\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))\ln t+b(\alpha,t),

for some error terms
a_{t}
and
b(\alpha,t)
fulfilling
|a_{t}|\leq\Cr{constapproxtopamwithspeedv0}\ln t
and

|b(\alpha,t)|\leq\Cr{constapproxtopamwithspeedv0}\ln t+\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}ert_{g}enau}+\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}ert_{g}enau}\cdot|\alpha\ln t-h_{t}|\cdot\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\ln t}{t}}+\frac{|\alpha\ln t-h_{t}|}{t}\Big)

for all
t
large enough.
Indeed, the first equality is due to Lemma
3.13
, the second due to the time perturbation Lemma
3.11
, the last one due to the identity
\texttt{es}-\overline{\eta}(v_{0})=v_{0}L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0})).

Then due to
|h_{t}|\leq c_{1}\sqrt{t\ln t}
for large
t
(cf. (
3.46
)), choosing
\Cr{constapproxofTn}=\alpha>\frac{2\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}ert_{g}enau}\cdot C^{2}}{\texttt{es}-\overline{\eta}(v_{0})}
the latter term tends to infinity, supplying us with (
3.48
).

To complete the proof, equation (
3.45
) is a direct consequence of (
3.44
) and (
2.9
). ∎

Recall the definition
\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}=\overline{m}^{\xi,u_{0},a}
from (
1.6
).

Corollary 3.15
.

For all
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
and
a>0
we have

\frac{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}{t}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}_{t\rightarrow\infty}v_{0}\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(3.49)

Proof.

For an upper bound, we have
\limsup_{t\to\infty}\frac{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}{t}=\limsup_{t\to\infty}\frac{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}{T_{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}^{u_{0},a}}\frac{T_{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}^{u_{0},a}}{t}\leq v_{0}
, where the last inequality is due to
T_{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}^{u_{0},a}\leq t
and (
3.45
). To get a lower bound, we
can use the properties of the Lyapunov exponent from Proposition
A.3
, giving
\liminf_{t\to\infty}\frac{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}{t}\geq v
for all
v\in(0,v_{0})
, and we can conclude.
∎

Lemma 3.16
.

For every
a>0
there exists a constant
=\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt}(a)>0
and a
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. finite random variable
=\Cr{timelemmaT_{m}teqt}(a)
such that for all
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
and
t\geq\Cr{timelemmaT_{m}teqt},

t-\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt}\leq T^{u_{0},a}_{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}\leq t.

(3.50)

Proof.

By definition, the inequality
T^{u_{0},a}_{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}\leq t
follows directly. To show
t-\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt}\leq T^{u_{0},a}_{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}
, recall that due to (
3.49
) we can use time perturbation. Indeed,
by defining
\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt}:=\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}\ln\big(\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}\cdot 3C^{\prime}/a\big)
with
C^{\prime}
from (
PAM-INI
) and
\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}
from Lemma
3.11
b), for all
t
large enough

\displaystyle u^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}(t-\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt},\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t))

\displaystyle\leq\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}e^{-\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt}/\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}}u^{u_{0}}(t,\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t))<\frac{a}{2C^{\prime}}

and thus, recalling
u^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}(s,x)=\texttt{E}_{x}^{\xi}\big[N^{\leq}(s,0)\big]
and Lemma
A.6
, we get the lower bound
T^{u_{0},a}_{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}\geq T^{C^{\prime}\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]},a}_{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}=T^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]},a/C^{\prime}}_{\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}(t)}\geq t-\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt}
for all
t
large enough and we can conclude.
∎

Recall definition (
3.43
) for
T_{x}^{(a)}
.

Corollary 3.17
.

There exists
\overline{K}\in(1,\infty)
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for all
a>0
and for all
x
large enough

\sup_{|y|\leq 1}T^{(a)}_{x+y}-\overline{K}\leq T^{(a)}_{x}\leq\inf_{|y|\leq 1}T^{(a)}_{x+y}+\overline{K}.

(3.51)

Proof.

We set
\overline{K}:=1+\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}\big(\ln(2\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation})+\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}\big)
. Then due to (
3.45
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
x
large enough we have

\frac{x+y^{\prime}}{T^{(a)}_{x+y^{\prime\prime}}\pm\overline{K}}\in V\quad\forall\ y^{\prime},y^{\prime\prime}\in[-1,1].

This allows us to apply the inequalities (
3.33
) and (
3.40
) for
u_{0}=\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}
. Indeed, for all
|y|\leq 1,

\displaystyle u(T^{(a)}_{x+y}-\overline{K},x)

\displaystyle\leq\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}e^{\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}}u(T^{(a)}_{x+y}-\overline{K},x+y)\leq\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}e^{\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}}\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}e^{-(\overline{K}-1)/\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}}u(T^{(a)}_{x+y}-1,x+y)<\frac{a}{2},

where the first inequality is due to (
3.40
), the second one due to (
3.33
) and the last one uses
u(T^{(a)}_{x+y}-1,x+y)<a
. By Lemma
A.6
we get
u(t,x)<a
for all
t\leq T^{(a)}_{x+y}-\overline{K}
and thus the left-hand side in (
3.51
). Analogously, first applying (
3.40
) and then (
3.33
), we have

\displaystyle u(T^{(a)}_{x+y}+\overline{K},x)

\displaystyle\geq\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}^{-1}e^{-\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}}u(T^{(a)}_{x+y}+\overline{K},x+y)\geq\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}^{-1}e^{-\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}}\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}^{-1}e^{\overline{K}/\Cr{const_{t}ime_{p}erturbation}}u(T^{(a)}_{x+y},x+y)\geq a

for all
|y|\leq 1
, giving the right-hand side of (
3.51
).
∎

Corollary 3.18
.

Let
\overline{m}^{a}(t)=\overline{m}^{\xi,\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]},a}(t)
,
a>0
, be defined in (
1.6
). Then for all
0<\varepsilon\leq M
there exists
C=C(\varepsilon,M)
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
t
large enough

0\leq\overline{m}^{\varepsilon}(t)-\overline{m}^{M}(t)\leq C.

Proof.

The first inequality is clear. By Corollary
3.15
, we can use the second inequality from Lemma
3.12
b) and get the claim by defining
C:=\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}\ln\big(\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}\cdot M/\varepsilon\big)
with
\Cr{const_{s}pace_{p}erturbation}
from Lemma
3.12
b) to get
u^{\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}}(t,\overline{m}^{\varepsilon}(t)-C)\geq M
and thus
\overline{m}^{M}(t)\geq\overline{m}^{\varepsilon}(t)-C
for all
t
large enough.
∎

3.6
Proof of Theorem
1.3

Using the preparatory results from the previous sections, it is now possible to obtain an invariance principle for the front of the solution to (
PAM
). Roughly speaking, up to some error which can be controlled by the results from the previous sections, we have
\overline{m}(t)\approx\ln u(t,v_{0}t)
and can then use the invariance principle from Theorem
1.2
to conclude.

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\text{PAM}}
,
a>0
and abbreviate
u=u^{u_{0}}
and
\overline{m}:=\overline{m}^{u_{0},a}
. We first assume
\sigma_{v_{0}}^{2}>0
. Then we have to show that the sequence of processes

[0,\infty)\ni t\mapsto\frac{\overline{m}(nt)-v_{0}nt}{\sqrt{n\widetilde{\sigma}_{v_{0}}^{2}}},\quad n\in\mathbb{N},

(3.52)

where
\widetilde{\sigma}_{v_{0}}^{2}>0
is given in (
3.57
) below,
converges in
\mathbb{P}
-distribution to standard Brownian motion in the Skorohod space
D([0,\infty))
. Notice that
[0,\infty)\ni t\mapsto\overline{m}(t)
might not be càdlàg
only
in
0
. To avoid this issue, above convergence is defined as convergence of the sequence of processes in (
3.52
) where we set
\overline{m}(t)\equiv 0
for
t
such that
\overline{m}(t)\leq 0
, making it càdlàg.

Due to the limiting behavior and the continuity of
x\mapsto u(t,x)
for
t>0
, the value
r(t):=\overline{m}(t)-v_{0}t
is the largest solution to

\ln u(t,v_{0}t+r(t))=-\ln 2.

We define

\mathcal{L}(t,h):=\ln\frac{u(t,v_{0}t+h)}{u(t,v_{0}t)},\quad t>0,\ h\in\mathbb{R},

U(t):=-\ln u(t,v_{0}t)-\ln 2=\mathcal{L}(t,r(t)),\quad t\geq 0,

let

\delta\in\big(0,|L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))|\big)

and
\varepsilon(t)
be a positive function such that
\varepsilon(t)\to 0
and
\varepsilon(t)t^{1/2}\to\infty
. Then by Lemma
3.12
, there is
C(\varepsilon)>0
such that for
t
large enough and all
h\in\mathbb{R}
fulfilling
C(\varepsilon)\ln t\leq|h|\leq\varepsilon(t)t
and
v_{0}+\frac{h}{t}\in V
we have

\displaystyle-\big(|L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))|+\delta\big)h\leq\mathcal{L}(t,h)\leq-\big(|L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))|-\delta\big)h.

(3.53)

Now, multiplying (
3.44
) by
v_{0}
, replacing
x
by
\overline{m}(t)
in (
3.44
) and recalling that
t-\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt}\leq T_{\overline{m}(t)}\leq t
by Lemma
3.16
, we get

\Big|(\overline{m}(t)-v_{0}t)-\frac{1}{L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))}\sum_{i=1}^{\overline{m}(t)}\big(L_{i}^{\zeta}(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))-L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))\big)\Big|\leq v_{0}\Cr{constprobapproxofTn}+\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt}+v_{0}\Cr{constapproxofTn}\ln(\overline{m}(t))

(3.54)

for all
t
large enough. Next, recall that
\frac{\overline{m}(t)}{t}\to v_{0}
by Corollary
3.15
and that the standardized sum
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{nt}\big(L_{i}^{\zeta}(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))-L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))\big)
converges in distribution to a non-degenerate Gaussian random variable by Lemma
3.2
. As a consequence, in combination with (
3.54
), we infer that
|r(t)|=|\overline{m}(t)-v_{0}t|\in[C(\varepsilon)\ln t,\varepsilon(t)t]
with probability tending to
1
as
t
tends to infinity. This and (
3.53
) implies

r(t)\in\Big[\frac{U(t)}{|L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))|\mp\delta},\frac{U(t)}{|L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))|\pm\delta}\Big],

(3.55)

with probability tending to
1
as
t
tends to
\infty
, where the upper sign is chosen if
U(t)>0
and the lower sign if
U(t)<0
. If
\sigma_{v_{0}}^{2}>0
, due to
\Lambda(v_{0})=0
and Theorem
1.2
, the sequence of processes

[0,\infty)\ni t\mapsto\frac{1}{\sqrt{nv_{0}\sigma_{v_{0}}^{2}}}U(nt),\quad n\in\mathbb{N},

(3.56)

converges in
\mathbb{P}
-distribution to standard Brownian motion.
Because (
3.55
) holds for all
\delta>0
small enough, Theorem
1.3
is a direct consequence of the convergence in distribution of (
3.56
) by choosing

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{v_{0}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{v_{0}}^{2}v_{0}}}{|L(\overline{\eta}(v_{0}))|}.

(3.57)

where
\sigma_{v_{0}}^{2}
is defined in (
3.1
). This gives the second part of Theorem
1.3
. If
\sigma_{v_{0}}^{2}=0
, we can proceed analogously and the first part of Theorem
1.3
follows from the first part of Theorem
1.2
and (
3.55
).
∎

4
Log-distance of the fronts of the solutions to PAM and F-KPP

We finally prove our last main result, Theorem
1.4
. In Sections
4.1
and
4.2
, we will assume that
u_{0}=w_{0}=\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}
. Indeed, using a comparison argument in the proof of Theorem
1.4
, it will turn out that this is actually sufficient for or purposes. It should also be mentioned here that the tools we employ are inherently probabilistic. As a consequence, and for notational convenience, we will mostly formulate the respective results in terms of the BBMRE in what follows below; the correspondence to the results in PDE terms is immediate from (
2.2
) and Remark
2.2
.

In the case
u_{0}=w_{0}=\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,0]}
, using Markov’s inequality we infer

\texttt{P}_{x}^{\xi}\left(N^{\leq}(t,0)\geq 1\right)\leq\texttt{E}_{x}^{\xi}\left[N^{\leq}(t,0)\right]

and thus
\overline{m}(t)\geq m(t)
for all
t\geq 0
, which establishes the first inequality in (
1.13
). The rest of this section will be dedicated to deriving the second inequality in (
1.13
), i.e., that the front of the randomized F-KPP equations lags behind the front of the solution of the parabolic Anderson model at most logarithmically.
We introduce some notation and, recalling the notation
X^{\nu}
introduced before (
2.1
), start with considering certain “well-behaved” particles

\begin{split}N_{s,u,t}^{\mathcal{L},a}:=\big|\big\{\nu\in N(s):X^{\nu}_{s}\leq 0,H^{\nu}_{k}\geq u-T^{(a)}_{k}-5\chi_{1}(\overline{m}^{(a)}(t))\ \forall k\in\{1,\ldots,\lfloor&\overline{m}^{(a)}(t)\rfloor\}\big\}\big|,\\
&a>0,\ s,t,u\geq 0;\end{split}

(4.1)

(
4.1
)
here,
H^{\nu}_{k}:=\inf\{t\geq 0:X^{\nu}_{t}=k\}
, the random variable
T^{(a)}_{k}
has been defined in (
3.43
), and

\displaystyle\chi_{b}(x)

\displaystyle:=\Cr{constprobapproxofTn}+b(1+\overline{K}+\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt})+\Cr{constapproxofTn}(\ln x\vee 1),\quad x\in(0,\infty),\ b\in\mathbb{R},

(4.2)

where
\Cr{constprobapproxofTn}
and
\Cr{constapproxofTn}
have been defined in Lemma
3.14
,
\overline{K}
is taken from Corollary
3.17
, the constant
\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt}
from Lemma
3.16
.
We abbreviate
N_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}:=N_{t,t,t}^{\mathcal{L}}
and call the particles contributing to
N_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}

leading particles at time
t
.
Cauchy-Schwarz immediately gives

\texttt{P}_{x}^{\xi}\left(N^{\leq}(t,0)\geq 1\right)\geq\texttt{P}_{x}^{\xi}\left(N_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}\geq 1\right)\geq\frac{\texttt{E}_{x}^{\xi}\left[N_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}\right]^{2}}{\texttt{E}_{x}^{\xi}\big[(N_{t}^{\mathcal{L}})^{2}\big]}.

(4.3)

The next two sections are dedicated to deriving an upper bound for the denominator and a lower bound for the numerator
of the right-hand side, both for
x
in a neighborhood of
\overline{m}(t)
.

4.1
First moment of leading particles

The biggest chunk of this section will consist of proving the following first moment bound on the number of leading particles.
Recall the notation
\overline{m}^{(a)}(t)
from (
1.7
).

Lemma 4.1
.

For all
a>0
there exists
\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{1}(a)\in(0,\infty)
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for all
t
large enough

\inf_{x\in[\overline{m}^{(a)}(t)-1,\overline{m}^{(a)}(t)+1]}\emph{\texttt{E}}_{x}^{\xi}\big[N_{t}^{\mathcal{L},a}\big]\geq t^{-\gamma_{1}}.

Proof.

Let
a>0
. To simplify notation, we will omit the index
a>0
in the quantities involved and write
N_{s,u,t}^{\mathcal{L}}:=N_{s,u,t}^{\mathcal{L},a}
,
T^{(a)}_{x}:=T_{x}
,
\overline{m}^{(a)}(t):=\overline{m}(t)
from now on.

Let
A_{u,t}:=\{H_{k}\geq u-T_{k}-5\chi_{1}(\overline{m}(t))\ \forall k\in\{1,\ldots,\lfloor m(t)\rfloor\}\}
,
let
K
be such that (
3.16
) holds and set
\overline{t}:=T_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}
. We obtain for all
t
large enough that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\inf_{x\in[\overline{m}(t)-1,\overline{m}(t)+1]}\texttt{E}_{x}^{\xi}\left[N_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}\right]\geq\frac{\inf_{x\in[\overline{m}(t)-1,\overline{m}(t)+1]}\texttt{E}_{x}^{\xi}\left[N_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}\right]}{2\texttt{E}_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}^{\xi}\left[N^{\leq}(\overline{t},0)\right]}\geq\frac{c}{2}\frac{\texttt{E}_{\lfloor m(t)\rfloor}^{\xi}\left[N_{t-1,t,t+1}^{\mathcal{L}}\right]}{\texttt{E}_{\lfloor m(t)\rfloor}^{\xi}\left[N^{\leq}\left(\overline{t},0\right)\right]}\\
&=\frac{c}{2}\frac{E_{\lfloor m(t)\rfloor}\left[e^{\int_{0}^{t-1}\xi(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};B_{t-1}\leq 0,A_{t+1,t}\right]}{E_{\lfloor m(t)\rfloor}\Big[e^{\int_{0}^{\overline{t}}\xi(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};B_{\overline{t}}\leq 0\Big]}\geq c_{1}\frac{E_{\lfloor m(t)\rfloor}\left[e^{\int_{0}^{t-1}\zeta(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};B_{t-1}\leq 0,A_{t+1,t}\right]}{E_{\lfloor m(t)\rfloor}\Big[e^{\int_{0}^{\overline{t}}\zeta(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};B_{\overline{t}}\leq 0\Big]};\end{split}

(4.4)

here, the first inequality follows from the definition of
T_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}
, the second inequality is due to Lemma
A.7
, the equality follows using Proposition
2.3
and the last inequality is due to
\xi=\zeta+\texttt{es}
, as well as (
3.51
) which gives
\overline{t}=T_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}\leq T_{\overline{m}(t)}+\overline{K}\leq t+\overline{K}
. The numerator can be bounded from below by

\displaystyle E_{\lfloor m(t)\rfloor}\left[e^{\int_{0}^{t-1}\zeta(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};H_{0}\in[t-3\overline{K}-\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt},t-1],B_{t-1}\leq 0,A_{t+1,t}\right]

\displaystyle\geq E_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}\Big[e^{\int_{0}^{H_{0}}\zeta(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s}\left.E_{0}\big[e^{\int_{0}^{r}\zeta(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};B_{t-1-r}\leq 0\big]\right|_{r=t-1-H_{0}};H_{0}\in[t-3\overline{K}-\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt},t-1],A_{t+1,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq c_{2}E_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}\Big[e^{\int_{0}^{H_{0}}\zeta(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};H_{0}\in[t-3\overline{K}-\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt},t-1],A_{t+1,t}\Big],

where the second inequality is due to
\zeta\geq-(\texttt{es}-\texttt{ei})
and
P_{0}(B_{s}\leq 0)\geq 1/2
for all
s\geq 0
. Now using the inclusion
\{B_{\overline{t}}\leq 0\}\subset\{H_{0}\leq\overline{t}\}
in combination with
\zeta\leq 0
, we infer
E_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}\big[e^{\int_{0}^{\overline{t}}\zeta(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};B_{\overline{t}}\leq 0\big]\leq E_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}\big[e^{\int_{0}^{H_{0}}\zeta(B_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s};H_{0}\leq\overline{t}\big]
. Thus, recalling
\overline{\eta}(v_{0})<0
and (
2.5
), we can continue to lower bound (
4.4
) via

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[\overline{m}(t)-1,\overline{m}(t)+1]}\texttt{E}_{x}^{\xi}\left[N_{t}^{\mathcal{L}}\right]\geq c_{3}\frac{E_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}^{\zeta,\overline{\eta}(v_{0})}\Big[e^{-\overline{\eta}(v_{0})H_{0}};H_{0}\in[t-3\overline{K}-\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt},t-1],A_{t+1,t}\Big]}{E_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}^{\zeta,\overline{\eta}(v_{0})}\big[e^{-\overline{\eta}(v_{0})H_{0}};H_{0}\leq\overline{t}\big]}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq c_{4}\frac{P_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}^{\zeta,\overline{\eta}(v_{0})}\Big(H_{0}\in[t-3\overline{K}-\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt},t-1],\ H_{k}\geq t+1-T_{k}-5\chi_{1}(\overline{m}(t)),\,\forall 1\leq k\leq\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor\Big)}{P_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}^{\zeta,\overline{\eta}(v_{0})}\big(H_{0}\leq\overline{t}\big)}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq c_{4}P_{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}^{\zeta,\overline{\eta}(v_{0})}\Big(H_{0}\in[\overline{t}-2\overline{K},\overline{t}-\overline{K}-1],\ H_{k}\geq\overline{t}-T_{k}-5\chi_{0}(\overline{m}(t)),\,\forall 1\leq k\leq\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor\Big),

where the last inequality is due to
t\geq T_{\overline{m}(t)}\geq\overline{t}-\overline{K}
and
\overline{t}\geq T_{\overline{m}(t)}-\Cr{constantlemmaT_{m}teqt}-\overline{K}
(by (
3.51
) and (
3.50
)).
Now as we recall that
\frac{\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor}{t}\to v_{0}
, abbreviating
\eta=\overline{\eta}(v_{0})
,
n:=\lfloor\overline{m}(t)\rfloor
and thus
\overline{t}=T_{n}
,
we see that in order to finish the proof, it suffices to show that there exists
\gamma\in(0,\infty)
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
large enough,

P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(H_{0}\in[T_{n}-2\overline{K},T_{n}-\overline{K}-1],H_{k}\geq T_{n}-T_{k}-5\chi_{0}(n)\;\forall k\in\left\{1,\ldots,n\right\}\big)\geq n^{-\gamma}.

(4.5)

Using the notation

\displaystyle\widehat{H}^{(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle:=H_{k}-E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\left[H_{k}\right]\quad\text{ as well as }\quad R_{k}^{(n)}:=T_{n}-T_{k}-E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\left[H_{k}\right],

(4.6)

the probability in (
4.5
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(\widehat{H}^{(n)}_{0}\in[R_{0}^{(n)}-2\overline{K},R_{0}^{(n)}-\overline{K}-1],\widehat{H}^{(n)}_{k}\geq R_{k}^{(n)}-5\chi_{0}(n)\ \forall k\in\left\{1,\ldots,n\right\}\big).

(4.7)

In order to facilitate computations, we approximate the sequence
(R_{k}^{(n)})
by a stationary one, setting

\displaystyle\rho_{i}

\displaystyle:=\frac{L_{i}^{\zeta}(\eta)}{v_{0}L(\eta)}-(L_{i}^{\zeta})^{\prime}(\eta)=\frac{1}{v_{0}L(\eta)}\big(L_{i}^{\zeta}(\eta)-L(\eta)\big)-\big(E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}[\tau_{i-1}]-\mathbb{E}[E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}[\tau_{i-1}]]\big)\ \text{ and }\

(4.8)

\displaystyle{\widehat{R}_{k}^{(n)}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=k+1}^{n}\rho_{i},\quad k<n,

(4.9)

where
\tau_{i-1}=H_{i-1}-H_{i}
, and in the equality we used
E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}[\tau_{i-1}]=(L_{i}^{\zeta})^{\prime}(\eta)
and
\mathbb{E}[E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}[H_{k}]]=\frac{n-k}{v_{0}}
. Applying inequality (
3.44
) from Lemma
3.14
and using the identity
\mathbb{E}[E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}[H_{k}]]=\frac{n-k}{v_{0}},
we get that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\begin{split}\big|R_{k}^{(n)}-\widehat{R}_{k}^{(n)}\big|&\leq 2\left(\Cr{constprobapproxofTn}+\Cr{constapproxofTn}\ln n\right)\quad\text{for all $n\in\mathbb{N}$ and each $k\in\{0,\ldots,n\}.$}\end{split}

(4.10)

From now on we will write
\chi:=\chi_{0}
. Then by (
4.10
), the probability in (
4.7
) can be lower bounded by

\displaystyle P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(\widehat{H}^{(n)}_{0}\in[R_{0}^{(n)}-2\overline{K},R_{0}^{(n)}-\overline{K}-1];\widehat{H}^{(n)}_{k}\geq\widehat{R}_{k}^{(n)}-3\chi(n)\ \forall k\in\left\{1,\ldots,n\right\}\big).

(4.11)

Now, for every
n
, enlarging the underlying probability space if necessary, we introduce two processes
(B_{t}^{(i,n)})_{t\geq 0}
,
i=1,2
, which are independent from everything else and Brownian motions under
P_{n}
, starting in
n
, and, without further formal definition, we tacitly assume in the following that the tilting of the probability measure
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}
of our original Brownian motion
also applies to

(B_{t}^{(i,n)})_{t\geq 0}
,
i=1,2,
in the obvious way.
For
i=1,2
, let
H^{(i,n)}_{k}:=\inf\{t\geq 0:B_{t}^{(i,n)}=k\}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, be the corresponding hitting times,
\widehat{H}_{k}^{(i,n)}:={H}_{k}^{(i,n)}-E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}[{H}_{k}^{(i,n)}]
and let
\Sigma_{n}
be a random variable which, under
P_{n}
, is uniformly distributed on
\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
and independent of everything else. We define

\displaystyle\beta_{k}^{(i,n)}

\displaystyle:=\widehat{H}^{(i,n)}_{k}-\widehat{R}_{k}^{(n)},\quad k=n-1,n-2,\ldots,\quad i=1,2,

\displaystyle\beta_{k}^{(n)}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\beta^{(1,n)}_{k},&\Sigma_{n}\leq k<n,\\
\beta^{(1,n)}_{\Sigma_{n}}+\big(\beta^{(2,n)}_{k}-\beta^{(2,n)}_{\Sigma_{n}}\big),&k<\Sigma_{n}.\end{cases}

The
\xi
-adaptedness of the process
(\widehat{R}_{k}^{(n)})_{k<n}
implies that the processes
(\beta^{(i,n)}_{k})_{k<n}
,
i=1,2
, are
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}
-independent and have the same distribution as
(\beta_{k}^{(n)})_{k<n}
. We can therefore rewrite (
4.11
) as

\displaystyle P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\left(\beta_{k}^{(n)}\geq-3\chi(n)\ \forall k\in\left\{1,\ldots,n\right\},\beta_{0}^{(n)}\in I_{n}\right),

(4.12)

where
I_{n}:=\big[R_{0}^{(n)}-\widehat{R}_{0}^{(n)}-2\overline{K},R_{0}^{(n)}-\widehat{R}_{0}^{(n)}-\overline{K}-1\big].
Due to (
4.10
) we have that

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
n
large enough,
R_{0}^{(n)}-\widehat{R}_{0}^{(n)}-2\overline{K}\geq-3\chi(n),
i.e.

I_{n}\subset[-3\chi(n),\infty).

(4.13)

For each
k\in\{0,\ldots,n\}
we introduce

\displaystyle\overline{\beta}^{(1,n)}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\beta^{(1,n)}_{n-1-k}-\beta^{(1,n)}_{n-1},\qquad\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{k}:=\beta^{(2,n)}_{k}-\beta^{(2,n)}_{0},

and note that

\beta_{0}^{(n)}=\overline{\beta}_{n-1-\Sigma_{n}}^{(1,n)}-\overline{\beta}_{\Sigma_{n}}^{(2,n)}+\beta_{n-1}^{(1,n)}.

(4.14)

An illustration of the various processes introduced above is given in Figure
2
below.
Now the key to bound the probability in (
4.12
) is the following lemma.

Lemma 4.2
.

(a)

There exists
\gamma^{\prime}<\infty
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
n
large enough,

\displaystyle P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(\overline{\beta}^{(1,n)}_{k}\geq 0\ \forall 0\leq k\leq n,\ \overline{\beta}^{(1,n)}_{n}\geq n^{1/4}\big)

\displaystyle\geq n^{-\gamma^{\prime}},\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{k}\geq 0\ \forall 0\leq k\leq n,\ \overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{n}\geq n^{1/4}\big)

\displaystyle\geq n^{-\gamma^{\prime}}.

(b)

There exists
C(\gamma^{\prime})>0
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
n
large enough,

\displaystyle P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\Big(\max_{1\leq k\leq n,i\in\{1,2\}}\big|\beta_{k}^{(i,n)}-\beta_{k-1}^{(i,n)}\big|\leq C(\gamma^{\prime})\ln n\Big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-3\gamma^{\prime}}.

(4.15)

(c)

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
. There exists
c>0
such that for all
x\geq 1
and all
n\in\mathbb{Z},

\displaystyle P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(\beta_{n-1}^{(1,n)}\in[x,x+\delta]\big)\geq c\delta e^{-x/c}.

Before proving Lemma
4.2
we will finish the current proof in order not to interrupt the Reader. To this end let

J_{n}:=\sup\big\{k\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}\,:\,I_{n}-\overline{\beta}_{n-k+1}^{(1,n)}+\overline{\beta}_{k}^{(2,n)}\subset[0,2C(\gamma^{\prime})\ln n]\big\},

where as always
\sup\emptyset:=-\infty
. We have

\displaystyle\left\{\beta_{k}^{(n)}\geq-3\chi(n)\ \forall 0\leq k\leq n-1,\ \beta_{0}^{(n)}\in I_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\supset\Big(\big\{\overline{\beta}^{(1,n)}_{k}\geq 0\ \forall 0\leq k\leq n,\ \overline{\beta}^{(1,n)}_{n}\geq n^{1/4}\big\}\cap\big\{\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\big|\overline{\beta}_{k}^{(1,n)}-\overline{\beta}_{k-1}^{(1,n)}\big|\leq C(\gamma^{\prime})\ln n\big\}\Big.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cap\big\{\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{k}\geq 0\ \forall 0\leq k\leq n,\ \overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{n}\geq n^{1/4}\big\}\cap\big\{\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\big|\overline{\beta}_{k}^{(2,n)}-\overline{\beta}_{k-1}^{(2,n)}\big|\leq C(\gamma^{\prime})\ln n\big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\Big.\cap\big\{\beta_{n-1}^{(1,n)}\in I_{n}-\overline{\beta}_{n-1-\Sigma_{n}}^{(1,n)}+\overline{\beta}_{\Sigma_{n}}^{(2,n)}\big\}\cap\{\Sigma_{n}=J_{n}\}\Big).

(4.16)

Indeed, due to (
4.14
), the fifth event on the right-hand side of (
4.16
) entails that
\beta_{0}^{(n)}\in I_{n}
must hold.
On the last two events on the right-hand side of (
4.16
) we have
\beta^{(1,n)}_{n-1}\geq 0
and thus the first event on the right-hand side of (
4.16
) implies that
\beta^{(n)}_{k}
is non-negative for
k\geq\Sigma_{n}
. The third event then implies monotonicity at times
k<\Sigma_{n}
. Since
I_{n}\subset[-3\chi(n),\infty)
due to (
4.13
), this gives the first condition on the left-hand side of (
4.16
).

Figure 2:
Illustration of (
4.16
).

Now the first and third event on the right hand-side of (
4.16
) are independent under
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}
and their probabilities are bounded from below by
n^{-\gamma^{\prime}}
due to Lemma
4.2
a). Thus, as a consequence of Lemma
4.2
b), for
n
large enough the probability of the first four events is bounded from below by
n^{-2\gamma^{\prime}}-n^{-3\gamma^{\prime}}
.
Furthermore, the first four events imply that
J_{n}\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
. Thus, due to Lemma
4.2
, conditionally on the occurrence of the first four events, the probability of the last two events on the right-hand side in (
4.16
) can be bounded from below by
cn^{-1}e^{-C(\gamma^{\prime})\ln n/c}\geq n^{-\gamma^{\prime\prime}}
for
n
large enough. The proof of (
4.5
) and thus of Lemma
4.1
is completed by the choice
\gamma_{1}>2\gamma^{\prime}+\gamma^{\prime\prime}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

(b)
Because
H_{k}^{(1,n)}-H_{k-1}^{(1,n)}\overset{d}{=}\tau_{k-1}
under
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}
, by recalling
\mathbb{E}\big[E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}[\tau_{k}]\big]=\frac{1}{n}
and the definition of
\widehat{R}_{k}^{(n)}
from (
4.8
), we have
\beta_{k}^{(1,n)}-\beta_{k-1}^{(1,n)}=\tau_{k-1}-\frac{L_{k}^{\zeta}(\eta)}{v_{0}L(\eta)}
. Now
L_{k}^{\zeta}(\eta)
is
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. bounded by Lemma
A.1
. Furthermore, for all
\theta
such that
|\theta|\leq|\eta^{*}|
(where
\triangle=[\eta_{*},\eta^{*}]
), we have

0\leq E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big[e^{\theta\tau_{k-1}}\big]=E_{k}^{\zeta,\eta}\big[e^{\theta\tau_{k-1}}\big]\leq\left(E_{k}\big[e^{(-\texttt{es}+\texttt{ei}+\eta_{*})H_{k-1}}\big]\right)^{-1}=e^{\sqrt{2(\texttt{es}-\texttt{ei}+|\eta_{*}|)}}<\infty,

where the last equation is due to
[
4
, (2.0.1), p. 204]
. I.e.,
\tau_{k}
has uniform exponential moments under
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}
and thus (
4.15
) follows by a union bound in combination with the exponential Chebyshev inequality.

(c)
We have
\beta_{n-1}^{(1,n)}=H_{n-1}^{(1,n)}-E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}[H_{n-1}^{(1,n)}]-\widehat{R}_{n-1}^{(n)}=H_{n-1}^{(1,n)}-E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}[\tau_{n-1}]-\rho_{n}
, thus recalling definition (
4.8
), the event in (c) is equivalent to
\{H_{n-1}^{(1,n)}\in[x,x+\delta]+\frac{L_{n}^{\zeta}(\eta)}{v_{0}L(\eta)}\}
. Because
\frac{L_{n}^{\zeta}(\eta)}{v_{0}L(\eta)}
is uniformly bounded and non-negative, it suffices to check that for every
C>0,
there exists
c>0
such that
\inf_{y\in[0,C]}P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}(H_{n-1}^{(1,n)}\in[x+y,x+y+\delta])\geq c\delta e^{-x/c}
for all
x\geq 1
. Indeed, recalling (
2.5
), we can lower bound

\displaystyle P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}(H_{n-1}^{(1,n)}\in[x+y,x+y+\delta])\geq E_{n}\big[e^{-(\texttt{es}-\texttt{ei}-\eta_{*})H_{n-1}};H_{n-1}^{(1,n)}\in[x+y,x+y+\delta]\big]

\displaystyle\quad\geq e^{-(\texttt{es}-\texttt{ei}-\eta_{*})(x+y+\delta)}P_{n}\big(H_{n-1}^{(1,n)}\in[x+y,x+y+\delta]\big)\geq\frac{\delta e^{-(\texttt{es}-\texttt{ei}-\eta_{*})(x+y+\delta)}}{\sqrt{2\pi(x+y+\delta)^{3}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2(x+y)}},

where the last inequality is due to
[
4
, (2.0.2), p. 204]
.
Now since the latter term can be lower bounded by
c\delta e^{-x/c}
, uniformly in
y\in[0,C],
the claim follows.

(a)
We will prove the second inequality, and explain the modifications that are necessary to show the first one at the end of the proof. For later reference it will serve our purposes to exclude some potentially bad behavior of the process
(\widehat{R}_{k}^{(n)}-\widehat{R}_{k-1}^{(n)})_{k}
. To do so, we take advantage of the next claim, the proof of which we provide after concluding the proof of Lemma
4.2
.

Claim 4.3
.

For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the sequence

(\rho_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
consists of
\mathbb{P}
-centered and
\mathbb{P}
-stationary random variables, and the family
(\rho_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
is bounded
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. In addition,
\rho_{i}
is
\mathcal{F}^{i-1}
-adapted and there exists
>0
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for all
k,n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
k<n
, we have

\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\rho_{n-k}|\mathcal{F}^{n}\big]\big|\leq\Cr{const_{p}ho}\cdot\big(\psi(k/2)+e^{-k/\Cr{const_{p}ho}}\big).

(4.17)

Furthermore, there exists
\overline{\sigma}\in[0,\infty)
such that
n^{-1/2}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\rho_{l}
and
n^{-1/2}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\rho_{-l}
converge in
\mathbb{P}
-distribution to
\overline{\sigma}X
as
n\to\infty
, where
X\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
is a standard Normal random variable.

Now due to (
4.17
),
(\rho_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
fulfills the conditions of Corollary
A.5
. As a consequence we deduce that
for
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\geq 0
we have

\mathbb{P}\big(\sum_{l=1}^{k}{\rho}_{l}\geq x\big)\leq c_{1}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{c_{1}k}},

which, using stationarity, can be extended to the maximal inequality (e.g. by
[
22
, Theorem 1]
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{0\leq k\leq y}\big(\widehat{R}_{r+k}^{(n)}-\widehat{R}_{r}^{(n)}\big)\geq x\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{0\leq k\leq y}\sum_{l=0}^{k}{\rho}_{l}\geq x\Big)\leq c_{2}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{c_{2}y}}\quad\forall r,y\in\mathbb{Z},\ x\geq 0.

(4.18)

Furthermore, recalling (
4.6
), (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), the increments of the process
(\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{k})_{k}
can be written as

\begin{split}\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{k}-\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{k-1}&=\big(H_{k}-H_{k-1}-E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}[H_{k}-H_{k-1}]\big)-\Big(\sum_{i=k+1}^{n}\rho_{i}-\sum_{i=k}^{n}\rho_{i}\Big)\\
&=\big(-\tau_{k-1}+(L_{k}^{\zeta})^{\prime}(\eta)\big)-\Big(-\frac{L_{k}^{\zeta}(\eta)}{v_{0}L(\eta)}+(L_{k}^{\zeta})^{\prime}(\eta)\Big)=\frac{L_{k}^{\zeta}(\eta)}{v_{0}L(\eta)}-\tau_{k-1}.\end{split}

\mathbb{P}
-a.s., by Lemma
A.1
b), the last fraction in the previous display is positive and uniformly bounded away from zero and infinity, whereas under
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}
,
\tau_{k-1}
is an absolutely continuous random variable with positive density on
(0,\infty)
.
Therefore, for the constant

a:=\frac{1}{4}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\essinf_{\xi}\big(\overline{\beta}_{k}^{(2,n)}-\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{k-1}\big),

we have
\essinf_{k,n\in\mathbb{Z}:\ k\leq n,\ \xi}P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}(\overline{\beta}_{k}^{(n)}-\overline{\beta}_{k-1}^{(n)}\geq 2a)\geq\delta
for some universal constant

\delta\in(0,1)
. We now split the environment into
\overline{\xi}(j):=(\xi(l))_{l\geq j}
and
\underline{\xi}(j):=(\xi(l))_{l<j}
and set
t_{0}=t_{-1}:=0
as well as
t_{i}:=2^{i}
for
i\geq 1
. Furthermore, we introduce two constants:
\overline{c}>0
, which is defined in (
4.31
) below, and
\overline{C}>0
, which is independent of
\overline{c}
and will be chosen large enough such that the sums in (
4.21
) and (
4.35
) below are finite. For
i\geq 1
, we define the random variables

\displaystyle\begin{split}Z_{i}^{(n)}&:=\underset{\overline{\xi}(t_{i+1})}{\text{ess inf}}\ \underset{x\geq at_{i-1}^{1/2}}{\inf}P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{t_{i}}\geq at_{i}^{1/2},\overline{\beta}_{k}^{(2,n)}\geq t_{i}^{1/4}\ \forall k\in\{t_{i-1},\ldots,t_{i}\}\ \big|\ \overline{\beta}_{t_{i-1}}^{(2,n)}=x\big)\\
&=\underset{\overline{\xi}(t_{i+1})}{\text{ess inf}}\ P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{t_{i}}\geq at_{i}^{1/2},\overline{\beta}_{k}^{(2,n)}\geq t_{i}^{1/4}\ \forall k\in\{t_{i-1},\ldots,t_{i}\}\ \big|\ \overline{\beta}_{t_{i-1}}^{(2,n)}=at_{i-1}^{1/2}\big),\end{split}

(4.19)

where
\underset{\overline{\xi}(x)}{\text{ess inf}}
means taking the essential infimum with respect to
\overline{\xi}(x),

and where the second equality is due to the monotonicity of the first probability in (
4.19
) as a function in
x.

Thus, as a random variable,
Z_{i}^{(n)}
is measurable with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{t_{i+1}}
. Now since
\overline{\beta}_{k}^{(2,n)}
is
\mathcal{F}^{k}
-measurable, we have that
Z_{i}^{(n)}
is
(\mathcal{F}^{t_{i-1}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{t_{i+1}})
-measurable. Setting
i(n):=\ln_{2}\big(\lfloor\big(\overline{C}\ln n\big)^{2}\rfloor\big)
,
we further define

\displaystyle Y^{(n)}

\displaystyle:=P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\Big(\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{k}\geq 0\ \forall\lfloor\overline{C}\ln n\rfloor\leq k\leq t_{i(n)},\ \overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{t_{i(n)}}\geq a\lfloor\overline{C}\ln n\rfloor\ \Big|\ \overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{\lfloor\overline{C}\ln n\rfloor}=2a\lfloor\overline{C}\ln n\rfloor\Big).

Writing
j(n):=\lceil\log_{2}(n)\rceil
, due to the Markov property of the process
\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}
under
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}
, we have
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely that for all
n
large enough,

\displaystyle P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\left(\overline{\beta}_{n}^{(2,n)}\geq n^{1/4},\overline{\beta}_{k}^{(2,n)}\geq 0\ \forall k\leq n\right)

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{k=1}^{\lfloor\overline{C}\ln n\rfloor}P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{k}-\overline{\beta}^{(2,n)}_{k-1}\geq 2a\big)\cdot Y^{(n)}\cdot\prod_{i=i(n)+1}^{j(n)}Z_{i}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\geq\delta^{\lfloor\overline{C}\ln n\rfloor}\cdot Y^{(n)}\cdot\exp\Big\{\sum_{i=i(n)+1}^{j(n)}\ln Z_{i}^{(n)}\mathds{1}_{B_{i}^{(n)}}\Big\},

(4.20)

where the event

\displaystyle B_{i}^{(n)}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\max_{{r\in[t_{i-1},t_{i}],\ 0\leq k\leq\frac{5}{2}at_{i}^{1/2}/\overline{c}}}\big(\widehat{R}_{r+k}-\widehat{R}_{r}\big)<at_{i-1}^{1/2}/16\Big\}

occurs
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely for all
i\in[i(n),j(n)]
and all
n
large enough.
Indeed, by (
4.18
) we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\sum_{i=i(n)}^{j(n)}\mathbb{P}\big((B_{i}^{(n)})^{c}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n}\sum_{i=i(n)}^{j(n)}t_{i-1}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{0\leq k\leq\frac{5}{2}at_{i}^{1/2}/\overline{c}}\big(\widehat{R}_{k}-\widehat{R}_{0}\big)\geq at_{i-1}^{1/2}/16\Big)

\displaystyle\leq c_{3}\sum_{n}n\sum_{i=i(n)}^{j(n)}e^{-a^{2}\overline{c}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/c_{3}}\leq c_{4}\sum_{n}n\log_{2}(n)e^{-a^{2}\overline{C}\ln n/c_{4}}<\infty,

(4.21)

where the last inequality holds true for
\overline{C}
large enough. Thus, the Borel-Cantelli lemma implies that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for all
n
large enough the events
B_{i}^{(n)}
occur for all
i\in[i(n),j(n)]
.
Furthermore, it is possible to show that
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, for all
n
large enough we have
Y^{(n)}\geq n^{-\gamma^{\prime\prime}}
. We postpone a proof of this fact, because it uses the same arguments as the following paragraph and we will describe necessary adaptations afterwards, cf. page
4.1
. Thus, for the time being it remains to show that there exists
\widetilde{c}>0
such that
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, for all
n
large enough,

\sum_{i=i(n)}^{j(n)}\ln(Z_{i}^{(n)})\mathds{1}_{B_{i}^{(n)}}\geq-\widetilde{c}\cdot j(n).

(4.22)

The second inequality in Lemma
4.2
(a) then follows from (
4.20
) with
\gamma^{\prime}>\overline{C}\ln(1/\delta)+\gamma^{\prime\prime}+\widetilde{c}/\ln(2)
.

In order to establish (
4.22
), it is enough to show that there exist
c^{\prime\prime},\theta>0
, independent of
\widetilde{c}
, such that for all
i
large enough,

\sup_{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{-\theta\ln(Z_{i}^{(n)})\mathds{1}_{B_{i}^{(n)}}}\Big]\leq c^{\prime\prime}.

(4.23)

Indeed, if (
4.23
) holds, setting
\widetilde{Z}_{i}^{(n)}:=\ln(Z_{i}^{(n)})\mathds{1}_{B_{i}^{(n)}}
, by Markov’s inequality we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{i=i(n)}^{j(n)}\widetilde{Z}_{i}^{(n)}<-\widetilde{c}\cdot j(n)\Big)\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{3}\sum_{i=\lceil i(n)/4\rceil}^{\lfloor\frac{j(n)}{4}\rfloor-1}\widetilde{Z}_{4i+k}^{(n)}<-\widetilde{c}\cdot j(n)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{k=0}^{3}\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{i=\lceil i(n)/4\rceil}^{\lfloor\frac{j(n)}{4}\rfloor-1}\widetilde{Z}_{4i+k}^{(n)}<-\widetilde{c}\cdot j(n)/4\Big)\leq 4e^{-\theta\widetilde{c}\cdot j(n)/4}\max_{k=1,\ldots,4}\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{-\theta\sum_{i=\lceil i(n)/4\rceil}^{\lfloor\frac{j(n)}{4}\rfloor-1}\widetilde{Z}_{4i+k}^{(n)}}\Big].

We will only estimate the above expectation for the case
k=0;
the cases
k\in\{1,2,3\}
can be estimated similarly. Now
\widetilde{Z}_{4i}^{(n)}
is
\mathcal{F}^{t_{4i-1}}
-measurable, hence, also recalling
t_{4i-1}-t_{4i-2}=2^{4i-2}
, by (
•
‣
1.4
) we have

\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\theta\widetilde{Z}_{4i}^{(n)}}|\mathcal{F}_{t_{4i-2}}\big]\leq(1+\psi(2^{4i-2}))\mathbb{E}[e^{-\theta\widetilde{Z}_{4i}^{(n)}}].

Since furthermore
\widetilde{Z}_{4(i-1)}^{(n)}
is
\mathcal{F}^{t_{4i-2}}
-measurable, we obtain via iterated conditioning that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{-\theta\sum_{i=\lceil i(n)/4\rceil}^{\lfloor\frac{j(n)}{4}\rfloor}\widetilde{Z}_{4i}^{(n)}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[\cdots\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\theta\sum_{i=\lceil i(n)/4\rceil}^{\lfloor\frac{j(n)}{4}\rfloor}\widetilde{Z}_{4i}^{(n)}}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{t_{4j(n)-2}}\big]\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{t_{4j(n)-6}}\big]\cdots\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{t_{2}}\big]\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=\lceil i(n)/4\rceil}^{\lfloor\frac{j(n)}{4}\rfloor}(1+\psi(2^{4i-2}))\mathbb{E}[e^{-\theta\widetilde{Z}_{4i}^{(n)}}]\leq(c_{6}c^{\prime\prime})^{j(n)},

for some
c_{6}>0
and
n
large enough. Choosing
\widetilde{c}
large enough, by a Borel-Cantelli argument similar to the proof of Lemma
2.5
, inequality (
4.22
) would follow.

Thus, in order to show (
4.23
), note that
because

Z_{i}^{(n)}=Z_{i}^{(n)}(\xi(\cdot))=Z_{i}^{(n-k)}(\xi(\cdot+k))\overset{d}{=}Z_{i}^{(n-k)}(\xi(\cdot))=Z_{i}^{(n-k)},

(4.24)

we can drop the supremum in (
4.23
).
In the following, we first choose
i
large enough (and from then on fixed) such that several estimates in the remaining part of the proof hold, and afterwards we adapt
n=n(i)
to ensure
0\leq i\leq\lceil\log_{2}(n)\rceil
. For simplicity, we write
Z_{i}:=Z_{i}^{(n)}
,
\beta_{k}:=\overline{\beta}_{k}^{(2,n)}
,
\widehat{H}_{k}:=H_{k}-E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}[H_{k}]
,
\widehat{R}_{k}:=\widehat{R}_{k}^{(n)}
and define

\displaystyle\overline{\rho}_{k}^{(j)}

\displaystyle:=\underset{\overline{\xi}(j)}{\text{ess sup}}\ \rho_{k},\quad\overline{R}_{k}^{(j)}:=\sum_{l=0}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{l}^{(j)},\quad 0\leq k\leq j.

Furthermore,
Thus,
\overline{\rho}_{k}^{(j)}
is
\mathcal{F}_{j}
-measurable and
\overline{R}_{k+l}^{(j)}-\overline{R}_{k}^{(j)}
is
(\mathcal{F}^{k}\cap\mathcal{F}_{j})
-measurable for all
l\geq 0
. Let
M_{R}:=\esssup\rho_{0}
and
L:=at_{i}^{1/2},
and note that the latter choice corresponds to diffusive scaling. Then we define

\begin{split}r_{0}&:=t_{i-1},\quad m:=\frac{L}{16M_{R}},\quad s_{0}:=\big(\inf\big\{k\geq r_{0}:\ \overline{R}_{k}^{(k+m)}-\overline{R}_{r_{0}}^{(k+m)}\geq L/8\big\}-1\big)\wedge t_{i},\end{split}

(4.25)

and for
j\geq 1
let

\displaystyle\begin{split}r_{j}&:=s_{j-1}+\Big\lceil\frac{L}{8M_{R}}\Big\rceil,\\
s_{j}&:=\big(\inf\big\{k\geq r_{j}:\overline{R}_{k}^{(k+m)}-\overline{R}_{r_{j}}^{(k+m)}\geq L/8\big\}-1\big)\wedge\left(r_{j}+(t_{i}-t_{i-1})\right).\end{split}

(4.26)

Heuristically,
s_{j}
is the first time after which the process
\overline{R}
(and thus
\widehat{R}
) increases at least by the amount
L/8
after time
r_{j}
. Such large increments of
\widehat{R}
are potentially troublesome, since as a consequence, the process
\beta
might decrease too much and cause the event in the definition of
Z_{i}
to have too small probability. In order to cater for this inconvenience, we start noting that by definition,
s_{j}-r_{j}
is bounded by
t_{i}-t_{i-1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{s_{j}+m}
-measurable, and
r_{j+1}-(s_{j}+m)\geq m.
Thus, by condition (
•
‣
1.4
), for every non-negative measurable function
f
we notice for later reference that

\mathbb{E}[f(s_{j}-r_{j})\,|\,\mathcal{F}^{r_{j+1}}]\leq\big(1+\psi(m)\big)\mathbb{E}[f(s_{j}-r_{j})].

(4.27)

Next, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{j}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\inf_{r_{j}\leq k\leq s_{j}}(\widehat{H}_{k}-\widehat{H}_{r_{j}})\geq-L/8,\ \beta_{s_{j}}\geq 2L\Big\},\quad\mathcal{G}_{j}^{\prime}:=\Big\{\inf_{s_{j}\leq k\leq r_{j+1}}(\widehat{H}_{k}-\widehat{H}_{s_{j}})\geq-L/8\Big\},

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle:=\inf\big\{j:s_{j}-r_{j}=t_{i}-t_{i-1}\big\}\wedge\inf\{j:s_{j}\geq t_{i}\},\quad\text{as well as}\quad\mathcal{G}:=\bigcap_{j=0}^{J}\mathcal{G}_{j}\cap\bigcap_{j=0}^{J-1}\mathcal{G}_{j}^{\prime},

and claim that

Z_{i}\geq P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(\mathcal{G}\ |\ \beta_{t_{i-1}}=at_{i-1}^{1/2}\big).

(4.28)

Indeed, on
[r_{0},s_{0}]
, the process
\overline{R}
(and thus also
\widehat{R}
) increases by at most
L/8
, and the process
\widehat{H}
decreases by at most
L/8
on
\mathcal{G}_{0}
. Moreover, for
j\geq 1
, on
[s_{j-1},r_{j}]
, the process
\widehat{R}
increases by at most
L/8
, and
\widehat{H}
decreases by at most
L/8
on
\mathcal{G}_{j}^{\prime}
. Finally, on
[r_{j},s_{j}]
, the process
\overline{R}
(and thus
\widehat{R}
) increases by at most
L/8
, and on
\mathcal{G}_{j}
,
\widehat{H}
decreases by at most
L/8
and
\beta_{s_{j}}\geq 2L
. All in all, conditioning on
\beta_{t_{i-1}}=at_{i-1}^{1/2}=L/\sqrt{2}\geq L/2
, we have

\beta_{k}\geq L/4\geq t_{i}^{1/4}
for
k\in[r_{0},s_{0}]
and
\beta_{k}\geq L
for all
k\in[s_{0},s_{J}]
. Since by definition,
s_{J}\geq t_{i}
, we get
\beta_{t_{i}}\geq L=at_{i}^{1/2}
, implying (
4.28
).

Furthermore, we can continue to lower bound

P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(\mathcal{G}\ |\ \beta_{t_{i-1}}=at_{i-1}^{1/2}\big)\geq P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\left(\mathcal{G}_{0}\ |\ \beta_{r_{0}}=L/\sqrt{2}\right)\prod_{j=0}^{J-1}P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\left(\mathcal{G}_{j}^{\prime}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{J}P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\left(\mathcal{G}_{j}\ |\ \beta_{r_{j}}=2L\right).

(4.29)

To see this, successively condition on
\beta_{r_{j}}\geq 2L,

j=1,\ldots,J,
and use the Markov property of the process
\widehat{H}
as well as the fact that
x\mapsto P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\left(\mathcal{G}_{j}\,|\,\beta_{r_{j}}=x\right)
is increasing. Then use the fact that under
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}
, the event
\mathcal{G}_{j}^{\prime}
is independent of
\beta_{r_{j}}
by the independence of the increments of
\widehat{H}
,
j=0,\ldots,J-1
.

In order to lower bound (
4.29
), observe that under
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}
, the sequence
(\widehat{H}_{k+1}-\widehat{H}_{k})_{k\geq r_{j}}
consists of independent and centered random variables, whose
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}
-moment generating function is finite in a neighborhood of zero. Thus, the central limit theorem
entails that for
i
large enough we have
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}(\mathcal{G}_{j}^{\prime})\geq 1/2
for all relevant choices of
j.

Moreover,

P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\left(\mathcal{G}_{j}\ |\ \beta_{r_{j}}=2L\right)\geq P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\Big(\widehat{H}_{s_{j}}-\widehat{H}_{r_{j}}\geq 5L/2,\inf_{r_{j}\leq k\leq s_{j}}(\widehat{H}_{k}-\widehat{H}_{r_{j}})\geq-L/8\Big).

We see that both events are nondecreasing in the (independent) increments of
\widehat{H}
. By Harris’ inequality (
[
5
, Theorem 2.15]
) we get

\displaystyle P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}

\displaystyle\Big(\widehat{H}_{s_{j}}-\widehat{H}_{r_{j}}\geq 5L/2,\inf_{r_{j}\leq k\leq s_{j}}(\widehat{H}_{k}-\widehat{H}_{r_{j}})\geq-L/8\Big)

\displaystyle\geq P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(\widehat{H}_{s_{j}}-\widehat{H}_{r_{j}}\geq 5L/2\big)\cdot P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\Big(\inf_{r_{j}\leq k\leq s_{j}}(\widehat{H}_{k}-\widehat{H}_{r_{j}})\geq-L/8\Big).

Recalling
s_{j}-r_{j}\leq t_{i}-t_{i-1}=\frac{L^{2}}{2a^{2}}
, a Gaussian scaling yields
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\Big(\inf_{r_{j}\leq k\leq s_{j}}(\widehat{H}_{k}-\widehat{H}_{r_{j}})\geq-L/8\Big)\geq c_{7}>0
.
To bound the first factor, we recall that by (
A.10
) and (
A.12
), we have
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

0\leq\overline{\rho}_{r_{j}+l}^{(r_{j}+k+m)}-\rho_{r_{j}+l}\leq c_{8}(\psi(m/2)+e^{-m/c_{8}})\quad\text{for all }l\leq k\leq t_{i}/2.

Because
m=\frac{L}{16M_{R}}
and
t_{i}/2=\frac{L^{2}}{2a^{2}}
, due to (
•
‣
1.4
) we finally get for all
i
(and thus
L
) large enough (due to
\psi(x)\cdot x\to 0
(
x\to\infty
), which itself is due to summability of
\psi(k)
), that

\begin{split}0&\leq\big(\overline{R}_{r_{j}+k}^{(r_{j}+k+m)}-\overline{R}_{r_{j}}^{(r_{j}+k+m)}\big)-\big(\widehat{R}_{r_{j}+k}-\widehat{R}_{r_{j}}\big)=\sum_{l=1}^{k}\big(\overline{\rho}_{r_{j}+l}^{(r_{j}+k+m)}-\rho_{r_{j}+l}\big)\\
&\leq c_{8}L^{2}(\psi(L/16M_{R})+e^{-L/c_{8}})\leq L/16\quad\text{for all }k\in\Big\{0,\ldots,\frac{L^{2}}{2a^{2}}\Big\}.\end{split}

(4.30)

By
s_{j}-r_{j}\leq t_{i}-t_{i-1}=\frac{L^{2}}{2a^{2}}
and (
4.26
), we see that
s_{j}-r_{j}\geq L/16
for all
i
large enough.
Recall that under
P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}
, the sequence
\widehat{H}_{s_{j}}-\widehat{H}_{r_{j}}
is a sum of independent centered random variables, whose moment generating function is uniformly bounded in a neighborhood of the origin. Then by
[
38
, (1)]
, we can apply
[
38
, Theorem 4]
in the following manner:
Let
c^{\prime}>0
be as in
[
38
, Theorem 4]
and in the notation of the latter theorem we choose
k=s_{j}-r_{j}
,
\alpha=\essinf_{\zeta,k<n}E_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}[(\widehat{H}_{k}-\widehat{H}_{k-1})^{2}]>0
,
M=|\eta|/2
,
u_{1}=\ldots=u_{k}=1
and

\overline{c}:=c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{|\eta|}{2}\cdot\alpha

(4.31)

Then a lower Bernstein-type inequality from
[
38
, Theorem 4]

gives that on
B_{i}^{(n)}
we have

P_{n}^{\zeta,\eta}\big(\widehat{H}_{s_{j}}-\widehat{H}_{r_{j}}\geq 5L/2\big)\geq c_{9}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{c_{9}(s_{j}-r_{j})}}.

(4.32)

Note that the condition in
B_{i}^{(n)}
makes
[
38
, Theorem 4]
applicable by ensuring ’enough’ summands
s_{j}-r_{j}
and is the main reason we have to introduce the sets
B_{i}^{(n)}
.
We will write
c=c_{7}\wedge c_{9}
from now on. Using (
4.28
) in combination with the lower bounds for the factors of (
4.29
) just derived, the term in (
4.23
) can be bounded from above by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\big[e^{-\theta\ln(Z_{i})}\big]\leq c_{10}\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp\Big\{\theta J\ln(2/c)+\theta\sum_{j=0}^{J}\frac{L^{2}}{c(s_{j}-r_{j})}\Big\}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq c_{10}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\frac{2}{c}\Big)^{\theta k}\exp\Big\{\theta\frac{L^{2}}{c(s_{k}-r_{k})}\mathds{1}_{s_{k}-r_{k}=t_{i}-t_{i-1}}+\theta\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{L^{2}}{c(s_{j}-r_{j})}\mathds{1}_{s_{j}-r_{j}<t_{i}-t_{i-1}}\Big\}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq c_{10}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{2}{c}\Big)^{\theta k}\big(1+\psi(m)\big)^{k}e^{\frac{2\theta L^{2}}{ct_{i-1}}}\cdot\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp\Big\{\frac{\theta L^{2}}{c(s_{j}-r_{j})}\Big\}\mathds{1}_{s_{j}-r_{j}<t_{i}/2}\Big],

(4.33)

where we recall
m
from (
4.26
), and the last inequality is due to (
4.27
) in combination with
t_{i}-t_{i-1}=t_{i}/2.

For the latter expectation we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\Big\{\frac{\theta L^{2}}{c(s_{j}-r_{j})}\Big\}\mathds{1}_{s_{j}-r_{j}<t_{i}/2}\right]=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(e^{\frac{\theta L^{2}}{c(s_{j}-r_{j})}}\mathds{1}_{s_{j}-r_{j}<t_{i}/2}\geq x\Big)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{2\theta L^{2}}{ct_{i}}}\mathbb{P}\Big(s_{j}-r_{j}<t_{i}/2\Big)+\int_{e^{\frac{2\theta L^{2}}{ct_{i}}}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(e^{\frac{\theta L^{2}}{c(s_{j}-r_{j})}}\geq x\Big)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.

(4.34)

Substituting
x=e^{\frac{\theta L^{2}}{cy}}
, the second summand can be written as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{i}/2}\frac{\theta L^{2}}{cy^{2}}e^{\frac{\theta L^{2}}{cy}}\mathbb{P}\left(s_{j}-r_{j}\leq y\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y.

In order to obtain an upper bound, we start with the probability inside the integral and get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(s_{j}-r_{j}\leq y\right)
